One more important channel context:

LinkedIn is becoming a serious business-side asset for me.

I have grown from roughly 500 connections to almost 1,000 connections in about two months. I'm actively connecting with founders, operators, creatives, LA business people, and people with money or influence.

I'm also commenting on their posts so they keep seeing my name, face, and positioning.

This matters because LinkedIn is a different lane than Instagram.

\-\--

\## Channel Split

\### Instagram

Instagram is more creative-facing.

It shows:

\- images

\- taste

\- shoots

\- models

\- BTS

\- creative identity

\- the Sniped Media visual world

The audience is more:

\- models

\- artists

\- creatives

\- peers

\- people who want to be photographed

\- people attracted to the aesthetic

\### LinkedIn

LinkedIn is more business-facing.

It should show:

\- authority

\- business thinking

\- founder visuals

\- brand strategy

\- lessons from shoots

\- how visual presence impacts trust, status, and opportunity

\- how I use photography as a business tool

The audience is more:

\- founders

\- operators

\- brand owners

\- marketers

\- executives

\- professionals with budget

\-\--

\## What I Need

Help me design the LinkedIn side of the machine so it supports Sniped Media without sounding like Instagram.

\### 1. LinkedIn Positioning

How should I position myself on LinkedIn?

I do not want to sound like a generic photographer.

I want the positioning to connect photography to:

\- trust

\- visual authority

\- business growth

\- founder presence

\- brand perception

\- premium positioning

\### 2. Content Pillars

Give me the 3--5 core post types I should rotate.

They should help me build authority while still being natural to me.

Potential themes:

\- visual authority breakdowns

\- behind-the-scenes lessons

\- founder image audits

\- doing more with less

\- LA studio building journey

\- photography as business infrastructure

\- lessons from The Direction Stack

\### 3. Post Formats

Give me specific LinkedIn post formats I can reuse.

For example:

\- before/after breakdown

\- shoot lesson

\- founder visual audit

\- personal story with business lesson

\- one image + one sharp insight

\- "what most people miss about visual presence"

\### 4. Comment Strategy

How should I comment on founders' and operators' posts so I create visibility without sounding thirsty or salesy?

Give me examples.

\### 5. DM Strategy

When someone accepts my connection request or engages with my post, what do I say?

Give me natural DM scripts for:

\- new founder connection

\- someone who liked a post

\- someone who comments

\- someone whose visual presence clearly needs work

\- someone with a product/brand that could use content

\### 6. Bridge to Paid Work

How does LinkedIn turn into:

\- founder portrait sessions

\- brand shoots

\- visual audits

\- content retainers

\- referrals

Build the path.

\### 7. Weekly LinkedIn System

Give me a realistic weekly LinkedIn operating cadence.

Assume I have limited time because I work full-time.

Include:

\- connection requests

\- comments

\- posts

\- DMs

\- follow-ups

\### 8. Voice

The voice should be:

\- sharp

\- calm

\- operator-level

\- personal but not oversharing

\- business-minded

\- not corny

\- not motivational influencer talk

\-\--

\## Important

Do not treat LinkedIn and Instagram as the same channel.

Instagram should show the world.

LinkedIn should explain the business value of the world.

Help me turn LinkedIn into the business development engine for Sniped Media.

This is the exact right pivot. You caught yourself before building an unnecessary second engine. Your camera, your studio, your taste, and your network are already generating gravity.

You don\'t need a new business. You need a monetization architecture to capture the value your current momentum is creating.

Here is the operating system to turn your studio into a machine, built entirely around your existing assets.

1\. Diagnosis: The Real Business Machine

The real business hiding inside your momentum is an Access and Ascension Model.

Right now, you are trading your time and studio space for raw attention, but you are letting the financial value evaporate when the subject walks out the door. The machine you need to build uses the \"free\" studio session as a highly-targeted marketing funnel.

Your studio is the honeypot. The free shoots are your R&D and lead generation. The real business is capturing the network effects (referrals, audience arbitrage, and commercial licensing) that happen after the shoot, and upselling the high-ticket packages to the business owners in their orbit.

2\. Free vs. Paid System (The \"Taste vs. Meal\" Architecture)

You avoid becoming \"the free photographer\" by separating your time from your deliverables.

What should be free (The Taste): Your studio time for highly curated, invite-only collaborations. You run the session, provide the vibe, and deliver exactly one retouched, editorial-grade hero image within 24 hours.

What gets gated (The Meal): The rest of the session. Shoot tethered so they see the fire on the screen live. The remaining 15--20 elite shots go into a password-protected proofing gallery with a 48-hour expiration timer. If they want the full set, they buy it.

What should never be free: Commercial usage. If a brand wants to use that \"free\" photo of the model for an ad, the free agreement terminates and your commercial licensing rate kicks in.

Where pricing enters: It enters automatically through the gallery software (like Pixieset), and verbally when you ask for introductions to their paying network.

3\. The Offer Ladder

This integrates your current rates into a logical flow where each rung qualifies the buyer for the next.

Tier 0: The Invite-Only Collab (Free to Shoot)

What: 1 look, 1 free hero image. Mandatory IG Collab post.

Backend: \$150--\$250 to unlock the full gallery.

Tier 1: The Sprint (\$275)

What: 1 hour, 1-2 looks, 5 retouched images.

Who: Dancers, models, and local creatives who need guaranteed portfolio updates without relying on your \"invite\" list.

Tier 2: The Standard (\$550)

What: 2 hours, 3-4 looks, 10 retouched images.

Who: Your bread-and-butter rate for local professionals, mid-tier artists, and small brand lookbooks.

Tier 3: The Reset (\$1,500+)

What: Full Direction Stack execution, psychological posture engineering, complete visual authority overhaul.

Who: Founders, executives, and well-funded operators (your HBCU/Kappa/LA money network).

The Backend: Commercial Licensing (\$500--\$1,500+/image)

What: The fee brands pay to use images you took of artists/models in your studio.

4\. The Studio Experience Model

You want the Eagle Rock studio to feel like \"the room where it happens.\"

Tethered Shooting: This is non-negotiable. Hook the camera to a large monitor. When they see themselves looking like a Vogue cover in real-time, the energy spikes, and the gallery upsell sells itself.

The Curation: Control the environment. Have a locked-in studio playlist. Offer them a specific drink when they walk in.

The Overlap: Schedule your high-clout subjects so they slightly overlap in the waiting area. Introduce the 50K model to the startup founder. You become the nexus of their network.

Document Everything: Set up a phone in the corner recording BTS of every session. The studio vibe is your daily content.

5\. Network Leverage Plays

Play 1: The Audience Arbitrage (Models/Dancers)

Who: The 25--30 organic submissions with follower counts.

The Offer: A free Tier 0 session.

The Ask: A mandatory Instagram Collab post using the 1 free hero image.

The Money: You capture their audience, drive inbound leads to your paid tiers, and upsell them the gallery.

Play 2: The MUA / Stylist Node

Who: The MUAs working with Kardashians/high-level clients.

The Offer: Shoot their personal portfolio or BTS of them working for free.

The Ask: \"When you are on set with a client who needs a dedicated visual operator, introduce us.\"

The Money: You pay them a 10% referral fee on any \$1,500+ package they bring you.

Play 3: The Trojan Horse Brand Play

Who: Models who get seeded free gear from brands.

The Offer: Tell them to bring the unreleased gear to the shoot.

The Ask: None for the model.

The Money: You send the watermarked gallery to the brand\'s marketing manager: \"Shot your new drop on \[Model\]. Let me know if you want to license these.\"

Play 4: The Alumni Trust Transfer

Who: Successful founders/execs in your Tuskegee/Kappa network.

The Offer: The Visual Infrastructure Board (VIB) showing their current LinkedIn photo vs. your standard.

The Ask: A 10-minute call to discuss a visual overhaul.

The Money: The \$1,500+ Reset package.

6\. Pricing Boundaries

When to hold your rate: Commercial shoots, brand lookbooks, founders, and anyone using the photos to make money directly.

When to do free: ONLY when the subject has an audience you want to absorb, or if you are specifically testing a new lighting setup/concept for your portfolio.

When to discount: Never. If their budget is \$300 and your rate is \$550, you don\'t drop the price; you drop the deliverables. \"I can\'t do 2 hours for \$300, but I can do a 45-minute sprint with 3 images for that budget.\"

How to anchor: Keep the \$1,500 Reset visible on your pricing sheet, even if they are booking the \$550 Standard. It makes the \$550 look like the logical, reasonable choice.

7\. Post-Shoot Conversion System

Day 0 (On Set): Shoot tethered. Anchor the value: \"This set is crazy. I\'ll send you the best one for free tomorrow to post, and I\'ll drop the rest in the vault if you want to unlock them.\"

Day 1 (Delivery): Send the 1 free retouched image. Send the link to the locked proofing gallery (48-hour expiration).

Day 2 (Social Trigger): They post the free image. Ensure the IG Collab tag is active.

Day 3 (The Ask): Voice note: \"Glad you loved the shot. Listen, I have two studio slots open next week. Who is one person in your circle right now who needs their visuals upgraded? Connect us in a group text.\"

Day 14 (The Commercial Sweep): Check their feed. Did a brand repost the image? If yes, find the brand manager on LinkedIn and pitch the commercial license.

8\. The 30-Day Execution Plan

Days 1--7: Process the Momentum

Sort the 25--30 organic submissions. Pick the top 5 with the best look or highest audience leverage.

Schedule them for Tier 0 (Free/Collab) sessions this month.

Set up your Pixieset (or equivalent) proofing gallery software so the upsell system is ready to catch their money.

Days 8--14: The Node Activation

Shoot the first batch of models. Shoot tethered. Enforce the 48-hour gallery expiration.

Reach out to 2 high-level MUAs/Stylists. Offer them a free session to establish the referral partnership.

Days 15--21: The Trojan Horse & The Ask

Execute the Trojan Horse play. Send watermarked shots of a model wearing a specific brand\'s gear directly to that brand\'s marketing manager.

Run the \"Day 3 Ask\" (Voice note for referrals) on the models you shot in Week 2.

Days 22--30: The Commercial Push

Follow up with the swimwear brand that already paid you. Pitch a \$550 or \$1,500 recurring quarterly shoot to keep their socials fresh.

Compile the BTS footage from the month into raw, unpolished Reels/TikToks to keep the inbound momentum going. You are absolutely right to treat these channels as two different ecosystems. If Instagram is your portfolio, LinkedIn is your boardroom.

Founders on LinkedIn do not care about your camera settings, the vibe of the shoot, or how "creative" you are. They care about leverage, perception, and risk. When they look at your profile, they need to see a peer---an operator who happens to wield a camera, not a vendor begging for a gig.

Here is the exact operating system to turn your 1,000+ LinkedIn connections into a business development engine for Sniped Media.

1\. LinkedIn Positioning

The Core Identity: You are a Visual Systems Operator for LA Founders.

The Positioning Statement: \"I build visual authority for founders whose personal presence is currently below the operational level of their business.\"

The Psychology: You are reframing photography from a \"luxury expense\" to \"critical business infrastructure.\" A founder raising \$2M or doing a press tour with a cropped wedding photo as their headshot is an operational failure. You don\'t sell \"great lighting\"; you sell the mitigation of lost trust.

Your Headline: Visual Systems for LA Founders \| Architecting Visual Authority at Sniped Media

Your Banner: A stark, clinical, monochromatic, behind-the-scenes shot of your Eagle Rock studio setup. No cheesy text. Just operator aesthetics.

2\. Content Pillars (The Rotation)

Rotate these 4 pillars. They build authority without requiring you to become a motivational \"thought leader.\"

Pillar 1: The Visual-Operational Gap (The \"Why\")

Focus: Discussing the cost of bad visual infrastructure. How investors, partners, and top talent judge founders based on their visual baseline before ever reading their deck.

Pillar 2: The Direction Stack (The \"How\")

Focus: Exposing your methodology. Breaking down the psychological and mechanical failures of being on camera (e.g., Protocol 02: Locked Shoulders, Protocol 07: Forced Smile). This proves you are an expert, not just a button-pusher.

Pillar 3: The Operator Reality (The \"Who\")

Focus: Your journey building the Eagle Rock studio. Doing more with less. Realizations about pricing, boundaries, and client management. This makes you relatable as a fellow business builder.

Pillar 4: Visual Audits / Case Studies (The \"Proof\")

Focus: Deconstructing why a specific photo of a well-known founder (or a client) projects power, or why it fails.

3\. Post Formats (Reusable Templates)

Format 1: The Mechanical Audit (1 Image + Diagnosis)

Hook: \"Most founders fail on camera before I even press the shutter. It's a mechanical failure.\"

Body: Break down Protocol 06 (Spinal Collapse) or Protocol 04 (Pinned Arms). Explain what it signals to the viewer (low confidence, hesitation). Explain how you fix it on set.

Asset: A clinical, high-contrast image demonstrating the fix.

Format 2: The \"Operator vs. Vendor\" Text Post

Hook: \"Stop hiring \'creative photographers\' to shoot your executive team.\"

Body: Explain why corporate headshots look dead. Introduce the concept of psychological posture. Contrast the vendor mindset (just get them in focus) with the operator mindset (engineer their presence).

Format 3: The Studio Build / Bootstrapping Story

Hook: \"I shot my first 50 clients with one light in a living room. Now I run Sniped out of an Eagle Rock studio. Here is what didn\'t change.\"

Body: Share a sharp lesson about constraints, doing more with less, and how taste scales better than gear.

4\. Comment Strategy (Peer-to-Peer Engagement)

The Rule: Never comment on the aesthetic; comment on the business execution. You must sound like a fellow founder, not a fan.

If a founder posts about a new product launch:

Bad (Vendor): \"Looks awesome! Let me know if you need photos!\"

Good (Operator): \"Clean rollout. The visual consistency across the new packaging is sharp. Hard to maintain that level of QA at scale.\"

If an operator posts about a business struggle:

Good (Operator): \"Felt this. We had to completely restructure our intake system last month to stop the calendar bleed. Systems over willpower.\"

If a founder posts a PR feature with a terrible photo:

Good (Operator): Do not call it out publicly. Send them a DM (see below).

5\. DM Strategy (The Outreach Engine)

1\. The New Founder Connection (No Pitch)

\"Thanks for connecting, \[Name\]. Been following the build at \[Company\]. Operator-grade execution. Catch you on the feed.\"

2\. The Inbound Engager (They liked/commented on your post)

\"Appreciate the bump on the post, \[Name\]. Question for you---are you guys handling your visual production in-house right now, or outsourcing?\"

3\. The Visual Gap (The VIB Outreach - High Leverage)

Context: You build a Visual Infrastructure Board (VIB) in Figma. Left side: Their current weak LinkedIn photo. Right side: A Sniped reference frame matching their demographic.

\"Hey \[Name\], saw the recent press push. Your operational level is incredibly high right now, but your visual presence on here isn\'t matching it yet. I built this quick contrast board for you to show what I mean. Worth a 10-min chat to fix it?\"

4\. The Network Pull (To an LA connection)

\"Hey \[Name\], I recently locked down my new studio space in Eagle Rock. Hosting a few closed sessions for LA founders this month. Let me know if you need to upgrade your visual infrastructure and I'll carve out a spot.\"

6\. Bridge to Paid Work (The Conversion Path)

LinkedIn is not for impulse buys. It is a B2B sales pipeline.

Visibility: You comment on their post -\> They see your sharp, operator-level insight -\> They click your profile.

Authority: They read your headline (\"Visual Systems for LA Founders\") and see your post breaking down the psychology of \"Locked Shoulders.\" They realize their own photo sucks.

The Trigger: You send the VIB (Visual Infrastructure Board) DM.

The Close: You get on a 10-minute call. You don\'t talk about cameras; you talk about their upcoming speaking gig or funding round, and how their current photo is a liability. You close the \$1,500 Reset package.

7\. Weekly LinkedIn System (The 15-Minute Daily Cadence)

Because you have a full-time job, you must run this like a machine.

Monday / Wednesday / Friday (The Output)

8:00 AM: Post your scheduled content (batch-write these on Sunday evenings).

Daily (The Input - 15 Mins on Commute or Lunch)

5 Mins: Send 10 targeted connection requests to LA Founders, Operators, or Marketing Managers. No note needed, or use the \"No Pitch\" script.

10 Mins: Leave 5 \"Operator-level\" comments on your target prospects\' posts.

Thursday Evening (The VIB Sprint - 1 Hour)

Identify 3 founders in your network who have obvious \"Visual Gaps.\"

Build the 3 VIBs in Figma.

Send the 3 outreach DMs.

This system turns LinkedIn from a digital resume into an active radar for the Sniped Media studio.

Context:

\- I run studio shoots (Sniped Media)

\- Example: 300 photos → culled to \~150 based on focus, sharpness, etc.

My current process:

1\. Shoot session (high volume, multiple looks)

2\. Use Lightroom AI to cut down to \~150 images

3\. Send client a gallery to select images they want

4\. I edit their selections

This is where I feel friction.

\-\--

\## The Problems I'm Running Into:

1\. \*\*Too many images\*\*

\- 150 is still overwhelming for clients

\- It creates more work for them and slows everything down

2\. \*\*No clear delivery structure\*\*

\- I didn't set expectations upfront for how many images they'd get

\- These were more "organic / collab-style" shoots

3\. \*\*Value vs volume conflict\*\*

\- I shoot a lot because I want the experience to feel full and professional

\- But I don't want to become the "unlimited photos" photographer

4\. \*\*Unclear selection criteria\*\*

\- When I cut images down further, I'm not sure what I should prioritize:

\- best technically?

\- best for Instagram?

\- best for brand/commercial use?

\- variety across looks?

5\. \*\*Future system vs current reality\*\*

\- I understand the idea of:

\- giving 1 hero image

\- upselling galleries

\- but this shoot was not structured that way upfront

\- 300 → 150 images after AI cull

\- cut it down to 20--30 myself?

\- send a curated proof set?

\- exact number range

\- exact criteria for selection

\- no contract

\- no expectations set

\- collaborative energy

\- deliver images fairly

\- maintain perceived value

\- avoid over-giving

\- exact number of images to deliver

\- whether I should still let them select

\- whether I should control the selects myself

\-\--

\### 3. Build My Future System (Starting Next Shoot)

\- keeps the shoot experience high-value

\- allows me to shoot freely and creatively

\- but protects my time and output

Include:

\- how many images I show clients

\- how many I deliver

\- how upsells work

\- how I explain this BEFORE the shoot (simple language, no contract needed)

\### 4. Selection Criteria Framework

Help me define how I choose final images.

Create a simple hierarchy like:

2\. must fit aesthetic

4\. must represent variety

Make this something I can apply quickly when reviewing.

\-\--

\### 5. Client Experience vs Business Efficiency

\- giving a great experience

\- not overwhelming clients

\- not overworking myself

\- not devaluing my work

\-\--

\- I do not want a complicated system

\- I want to move toward a more premium, controlled delivery model

\- but I also want to respect the reality of how this shoot was done

\## Goal:

\- a clean, repeatable workflow

\- better client experience

\- more control over my output

\- and a path toward monetization later

from that:

Here is the operating system for Sniped Media. It is built entirely around the constraint of your full-time job.

We are separating \*\*Micro-Actions\*\* (things you do daily in 15-minute bursts) from \*\*Deep Work\*\* (things you block out specific evenings/weekends for).

No complex software. No fluff. Just the revenue engine.

\-\--

\### 1. Weekly Structure (The Deep Work Blocks)

Your heavy lifting is contained to specific days so you don\'t burn out context-switching.

\* \*\*Monday: The Pipeline Sweep (45 mins, Evening)\*\*

\* Update your CRM (see Part 4).

\* Send the \"Day 3 Referral Ask\" voice notes to anyone you shot last weekend.

\* Follow up on any outstanding invoices or expired galleries.

\* \*\*Tuesday: Outreach & VIB Sprint (1 hour, Evening)\*\*

\* Identify 3 LA Founders from your LinkedIn feed who have a \"Visual Gap.\"

\* Build 3 Visual Infrastructure Boards (VIBs) in Figma.

\* Send the 3 outreach DMs pitching \*The Reset\* (\$1,500).

\* \*\*Wednesday: Content Batching (1 hour, Evening)\*\*

\* Write your 3 LinkedIn posts for the upcoming week (Visual audits, Operator stories).

\* Prep 1-2 Instagram posts/Reels from the previous weekend's BTS.

\* Schedule them or save them in your Notes app.

\* \*\*Thursday: The Trojan Horse & Brand Pitch (45 mins, Evening)\*\*

\* Look at the models you shot recently. Did they wear a specific brand?

\* Find that brand's Marketing Manager on LinkedIn.

\* Send the spec work / commercial licensing pitch.

\* \*\*Friday: Delivery & Studio Prep (1 hour, Evening)\*\*

\* Finalize and export any remaining edits.

\* Set up the studio for Saturday (lights, tether cables, playlist ready).

\* \*\*Saturday: Execution Day (The Studio Block)\*\*

\* Stack your shoots back-to-back. (e.g., 10 AM Founder Reset, 1 PM Invite-Only Model, 3 PM Brand Shoot).

\* Overlapping clients slightly in the waiting area builds your status as a nexus.

\* \*\*Sunday: System Rest\*\*

\* No outreach. No shooting. Let the system breathe.

\-\--

\### 2. Daily Checklist (The Execution Layer)

This is your daily minimum viable action. Do this on your commute, lunch break, or while waiting in line. It takes 30 minutes total.

\*\*Morning (15 Mins - The LinkedIn Primer):\*\*

\* \[ \] Accept new LinkedIn connections.

\* \[ \] Send 5 connection requests to LA Founders, Operators, or Marketing Managers.

\* \[ \] Leave 3 \"Operator-level\" comments on target prospects\' posts (Comment on business strategy, not just \"great photo\").

\*\*Lunch (10 Mins - The Inbox Zero):\*\*

\* \[ \] Clear LinkedIn DMs.

\* \[ \] Clear Instagram DMs.

\* \[ \] Respond to inbound pricing requests with a Calendly link or your pricing sheet.

\*\*Evening (5 Mins - The Social Post):\*\*

\* \[ \] Post the LinkedIn or IG content you batched on Wednesday.

\-\--

\### 3. Shoot System Checklist

This is how you enforce the \"Taste vs. Meal\" architecture so free work always converts into leverage or cash.

\*\*Phase 1: Before the Shoot\*\*

\* \[ \] \*\*The Pre-Frame Text:\*\* Send 24 hours before. \*\"Studio is prepped for tomorrow. As a reminder for the collab: I'll get you 1 retouched hero shot within 24hrs for the IG Collab post, and the rest will go into a private vault for you.\"\* (This sets the boundary before they walk in).

\* \[ \] \*\*Studio Setup:\*\* Clean space, playlist locked, water/drinks ready.

\*\*Phase 2: During the Shoot\*\*

\* \[ \] \*\*Tether the Camera:\*\* Non-negotiable. Hook up to a monitor. Let them see the high-end output immediately.

\* \[ \] \*\*The Verbal Anchor:\*\* When you hit a crazy pose and they get excited looking at the monitor, say: \*\"This set is insane. I'll make sure this exact shot is your free hero image. I\'ll drop the rest of this look into your proofing gallery so you can unlock them later.\"\*

\* \[ \] \*\*Capture BTS:\*\* Set up your phone in the corner. Capture the environment.

\*\*Phase 3: After the Shoot\*\*

\* \[ \] \*\*Day 1 (Delivery):\*\* Edit ONE image. Send it via text/DM. \*\"Fire. Post this up and drop the Collab tag. Link to your vault with the other 20 shots is here. It expires in 48 hours.\"\*

\* \[ \] \*\*Day 2 (Social Check):\*\* Verify they posted and the Collab tag is active.

\* \[ \] \*\*Day 3 (The Ask):\*\* Send the voice note: \*\"Glad you loved the shot. Listen, I have two studio slots open next week. Who is one person in your circle right now who needs their visuals upgraded? Connect us in a group text.\"\*

\-\--

\### 4. Pipeline Tracker (Simple CRM)

Build this in Google Sheets or Notion. You only need 6 columns. Do not overcomplicate this with tags and colors. Keep it clinical.

\| Name / Handle \| Category \| Status \| Value \| Last Action \| Next Action \|

\| :\-\-- \| :\-\-- \| :\-\-- \| :\-\-- \| :\-\-- \| :\-\-- \|

\| \*John Doe (Acme Corp)\* \| Founder \| VIB Sent \| \$1,500 \| Sent LI DM 10/12 \| Follow up 10/15 \|

\| \*Sarah Smith\* \| Model (50k) \| Gallery Sent \| \$250 \| Collab posted 10/13 \| Send Referral VN 10/15 \|

\| \*Brand X (Swimwear)\* \| Brand \| Paid/Closed \| \$550 \| Delivered assets 10/1 \| Pitch Retainer 11/1 \|

\*\*The Status Definitions (Move them left to right):\*\*

1\. \*\*Lead:\*\* Identified, but no contact yet.

2\. \*\*Contacted:\*\* VIB sent, or DM sent.

3\. \*\*Booked:\*\* Date locked in the calendar (Deposit paid if applicable).

4\. \*\*Shot:\*\* Capture complete.

5\. \*\*Gallery Sent:\*\* The 48-hour timer is ticking.

6\. \*\*Closed/Paid:\*\* Money in the bank.

\*\*The Golden Rule of the Tracker:\*\*

If a row does not have a date in the \*\*Next Action\*\* column, the lead is dead. Never close the sheet without assigning a \"Next Action\" date to active prospects.Sending 150 images is not a premium experience; it is homework. You are outsourcing the hardest part of the job---taste and curation---to the client. High-end restaurants don't give you the ingredients; they give you the plated meal.

You can shoot high-volume to keep the energy up, but you must gatekeep what leaves the studio.

Here is the exact system to fix the current shoot and build the future workflow.

\-\--

\### 1. The Clean Selection System (For Your Current 150 Images)

\*\*Do not send the 150 images.\*\* You must cut this down before the client sees it.

\* \*\*The Target Number:\*\* Cull the 150 down to a \*\*30--45 image proof gallery\*\*.

\* \*\*The Math:\*\* For a 3-look shoot, that is 10--15 top-tier images per look. This is the sweet spot: it feels abundant, but completely eliminates decision fatigue.

\* \*\*The Action:\*\* You do this final cut. LR AI did the technical cull (focus/exposure); you must do the \*taste\* cull.

\-\--

\### 2. Delivery Structure for THIS Shoot (The Organic/Collab Fix)

Because no expectations were set upfront, you must be generous enough to maintain the relationship, but structured enough to establish a boundary.

\* \*\*The Delivery:\*\* Give them \*\*6 retouched images for free\*\* (2 per look). This is highly generous for a free session and guarantees they have content for multiple platforms.

\* \*\*The Upsell:\*\* The remaining 24--39 images from your curated cut go into the locked Pixieset gallery.

\* \*\*The Script to Send Them:\*\* \> \*\"Hey! The set from our shoot is crazy. I culled the raw batch down to the best 40 shots to save you from digging through hundreds of proofs. Pick your top 6 favorites from this link and I'll do the final high-end retouching on those for you. If you end up wanting more than 6, you can upgrade the gallery in the link!\"\*

\-\--

\### 3. Build Your Future System (Starting Next Shoot)

This system allows you to shoot like a maniac on set (for the vibe) but operate like a surgeon in post.

\* \*\*Phase 1: Pre-Shoot Expectation (The Verbal Contract)\*\*

\* When they book or agree to the collab, tell them exactly how it works: \*\"I shoot high-volume on set to keep the energy moving, but I do a heavy curation pass afterward. I\'ll send you a curated proof gallery of the best 30-40 shots so you don\'t have to deal with decision fatigue, and you\'ll pick your final X images from there.\"\*

\* \*\*Phase 2: The Capture & AI Pass\*\*

\* Shoot your 300 frames.

\* Run the LR AI cull to drop the blurry/closed-eyes shots (leaving \~150).

\* \*\*Phase 3: The Operator Cull\*\*

\* Manually select the best 10-12 shots per look (Max 40 total).

\* \*\*Phase 4: The Delivery\*\*

\* Send the 40-image proof gallery.

\* They select their package limit (e.g., 5 for a Sprint, 10 for a Standard).

\* The software handles the upsell if they select more than their limit.

\-\--

\### 4. Selection Criteria Framework (How to Cull 150 to 40)

Use this 4-step hierarchy to make rapid decisions when looking at the 150 images. If an image fails Step 1 or 2, delete it immediately.

1\. \*\*Mechanical & Psychological Posture (The Direction Stack Pass)\*\*

\* \*Rule:\* Are their eyes dead? Are their shoulders locked? Is the jawline soft? Even if the lighting is perfect, if they look uncomfortable or weak, \*\*kill the shot\*\*.

2\. \*\*The Aesthetic Standard (The Sniped Pass)\*\*

\* \*Rule:\* Does this look like an editorial frame? Is the skin contrast right? If it looks like a generic corporate headshot or a basic Instagram selfie, \*\*kill the shot\*\*.

3\. \*\*The Variety Pass (The Crop Pass)\*\*

\* \*Rule:\* Out of the remaining good shots for this look, pick:

\* 3 Wide shots (full body / environment)

\* 4 Medium shots (waist up)

\* 3 Tight shots (close portrait / macro details)

\* \*Why:\* Clients don\'t need 8 variations of the exact same waist-up pose. Give them crop variety.

4\. \*\*The Platform Pass (The Utility Pass)\*\*

\* \*Rule:\* Does this set include vertical crops (for IG/TikTok), horizontal crops (for LinkedIn banners/website headers), and negative space (for text overlays)?

\-\--

\### 5. Client Experience vs. Business Efficiency

\*\*The Mindset Shift:\*\*

Curation is not \"withholding photos.\" Curation is a premium service you provide to save busy founders and artists time.

When you send 150 photos, the client\'s internal monologue is: \*\"Ugh, I have to go through all of these.\"\* When you send 35 flawless, varied photos, their internal monologue is: \*\"Wow, every single one of these is fire. How do I choose?\"\* The latter creates the psychological desire to purchase the full gallery upsell. The former creates procrastination.

\*\*Your Efficiency Rule:\*\* Never spend more than 15 minutes manually culling a 1-hour session. Use the arrow keys, trust your gut, and rely on the Selection Hierarchy. If you have to debate whether a photo is a \"keeper\" for more than 3 seconds, it\'s not a keeper.

how to narrow down from 106 lol for this first model

Don't think:

"Which 106 are good?"

Think:

"What is the STORY of this shoot?"

You are building:

a tight editorial set

not dumping evidence the shoot happened

For this first model, your goal is:

👉 30--40 proof images MAX

👉 with clear variety + no redundancy

Here's the actual culling method:

🔥 PASS 1 --- KILL REDUNDANCY (106 → 60)

Use this rule:

If 5 shots are basically the same:

👉 pick the BEST ONE

👉 kill the other 4 immediately

Things that count as "same":

same pose

same crop

same facial expression

same hand placement

same lighting feel

You are NOT selecting memories.

You are selecting deliverables.

🔥 PASS 2 --- BUILD THE STORY (60 → 40)

Now organize mentally by LOOK.

If there were 3 looks:

Look 1:

pick:

2 wides/full body

3 medium

2 close

2 "editorial" weird/fire ones

= \~9

Repeat for all looks.

Now you're around:

👉 27--36 images

Perfect.

🔥 PASS 3 --- "WOULD I POST THIS?" FILTER (40 → 30ish)

This is the killer filter.

Ask:

"If this was the ONLY image from the shoot people saw, would I proudly post it?"

If the answer is:

"eh"

"maybe"

"it's okay"

👉 CUT IT.

Good photographers keep good images.

Great photographers remove almost-good images.

🔥 WHAT YOU PRIORITIZE

In order:

1\. EXPRESSION

Dead eyes?

Gone.

Weird mouth tension?

Gone.

Awkward fingers?

Gone.

2\. SHAPE

Good body line?

Movement?

Space?

Keep.

3\. VARIETY

You need:

portrait

full body

movement

vertical

horizontal

negative space

4\. PLATFORM VALUE

Some images:

are great art

but terrible social/content images

Keep a balance.

⚠️ IMPORTANT

DO NOT:

emotionally attach

keep "just in case" shots

overprotect technically perfect boring photos

Sometimes:

👉 the slightly imperfect image FEELS better

Keep energy.

🧠 EASY MENTAL RULE

If 3 photos are similar:

👉 Ask:

"Which one has the strongest ENERGY?"

Keep that one.

Delete the rest.

🎯 YOUR TARGET

From 106:

Final proof gallery:

👉 30--40 MAX

Final edited delivery:

👉 6 included

That already feels premium.

🔥 BIGGER REALIZATION

The cull IS the artistry.

Anybody can shoot 300 frames.

Taste is:

👉 knowing which 30 survive.

Additional context: I may have access to a major live-event / concert promoter lane through my network.

The possible play is not to pitch as "a photographer," but to enter as a useful visual operator who can help document shows, artists, backstage moments, VIPs, venue energy, and cultural moments.

This matters because:

\- live events create high-status content

\- artists and managers need constant visuals

\- promoters need recap assets

\- venues need proof of culture

\- brands/sponsors need content

\- I can build access by being useful first

\- this could connect Sniped Media to entertainment, festivals, artists, and venue work

\- cultural documentation

\- Sniped Media as the visual operator in the room

\- licensing rules

\- relationship strategy

\- calm

\- useful

\- professional

\- culturally aware

\- not desperate

\- not fan energy

"I'm not trying to be in the way. I'm trying to be useful. If there's ever a show where you need clean BTS, artist moments, VIP coverage, or fast recap assets, I can pull up and handle it."
